AP Calculus AB - Unit 8 Canonical Content Bank

Integration Applications | Sections 8.1-8.5 | Canonical destination-first source

1. Notes Example / You Try It

Section 8.1 - Average Value and Net Change

U8-S1-NOTE-EX01
Section 8.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Find the average value of $f(x)=3 x^{2} - x + 3$ on $[1,4]$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{43}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: $f_{avg}=\frac{1}{b-a}\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$; substitute the interval and evaluate.
U8-S1-NOTE-YTI01
Section 8.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A quantity changes at rate $R(t)=2 t + 2$ units/hour. Find its net change from $t=1$ to $t=5$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $32$ units
Solution / scoring notes: $\int_a^b R(t)\,dt$ gives signed net change.
U8-S1-NOTE-EX02
Section 8.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A quantity changes at rate $R(t)=t + 4$ units/hour. Find its net change from $t=0$ to $t=1$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{9}{2}$ units
Solution / scoring notes: $\int_a^b R(t)\,dt$ gives signed net change.
U8-S1-NOTE-YTI02
Section 8.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $f(x)=x^{2} + 1$ on $[0,4]$, compute both the average value and the average rate of change and identify which uses a definite integral.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Average value $\frac{19}{3}$; average rate of change $4$.
Solution / scoring notes: Average value divides a definite integral by interval length; average rate of change uses endpoint function values.
U8-S1-NOTE-EX03
Section 8.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $f(x)=x^{2} + 1$ on $[0,3]$, compute both the average value and the average rate of change and identify which uses a definite integral.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Average value $4$; average rate of change $3$.
Solution / scoring notes: Average value divides a definite integral by interval length; average rate of change uses endpoint function values.
U8-S1-NOTE-YTI03
Section 8.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A tank contains 20 liters at $t=0$. Its net rate is $R(t)=3 t - 2$ liters/hour. How much is in the tank at $t=4$?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $36$ liters
Solution / scoring notes: Final amount = initial amount + net change = 20 + $16$.
U8-S1-NOTE-EX04
Section 8.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A tank contains 20 liters at $t=0$. Its net rate is $R(t)=2 t + 3$ liters/hour. How much is in the tank at $t=4$?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $48$ liters
Solution / scoring notes: Final amount = initial amount + net change = 20 + $28$.
U8-S1-NOTE-YTI04
Section 8.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A rate is tabulated at three equally spaced times. Use the trapezoidal rule to estimate net change.
$t$13/22
$R(t)$-1-34

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $-0.75$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply two trapezoids with the common half-interval width and preserve the sign of the rate values.

Section 8.2 - Motion with Integrals

U8-S2-NOTE-EX01
Section 8.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A particle has velocity $v(t)=t^{2} + t - 1$. Find its displacement on $[0,2]$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{8}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: $\int_a^b v(t)\,dt$ gives displacement.
U8-S2-NOTE-YTI01
Section 8.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A particle has velocity $v(t)=t - 1$. Find total distance traveled from $t=0$ to $t=2$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $1$
Solution / scoring notes: Split at the velocity zero and integrate speed $|v(t)|$.
U8-S2-NOTE-EX02
Section 8.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A particle has velocity $v(t)=t - 3$. Find total distance traveled from $t=0$ to $t=6$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $9$
Solution / scoring notes: Split at the velocity zero and integrate speed $|v(t)|$.
U8-S2-NOTE-YTI02
Section 8.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A particle starts at position $s(0)=5$ with velocity $v(t)=2 t - 2$. Find $s(3)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $8$
Solution / scoring notes: Add accumulated velocity to the initial position.
U8-S2-NOTE-EX03
Section 8.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A particle starts at position $s(0)=0$ with velocity $v(t)=2 t + 1$. Find $s(3)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $12$
Solution / scoring notes: Add accumulated velocity to the initial position.
U8-S2-NOTE-YTI03
Section 8.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $v(t)=t^{2} - 6 t + 8$ on $[1,5]$, find both displacement and total distance.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Displacement $\frac{4}{3}$; total distance $4$.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the signed integral for displacement and split at each velocity zero for total distance.
U8-S2-NOTE-EX04
Section 8.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $v(t)=t^{2} - 4 t + 3$ on $[0,4]$, find both displacement and total distance.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Displacement $\frac{4}{3}$; total distance $4$.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the signed integral for displacement and split at each velocity zero for total distance.
U8-S2-NOTE-YTI04
Section 8.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The figure shows velocity versus time as connected line segments. Find the displacement over the displayed interval.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{9}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Compute signed trapezoid/triangle areas under the velocity graph.

Section 8.3 - Area Between Curves

U8-S3-NOTE-EX01
Section 8.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Find the area of the region between $y=\frac{373}{64}$ and $y=\frac{5 x^{2}}{4} + 5 x + 7$ from $x=- \frac{15}{4}$ to $x=- \frac{1}{4}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{1715}{192}$
Solution / scoring notes: $\int_a^b(\text{upper}-\text{lower})\,dx$ gives geometric area.
U8-S3-NOTE-YTI01
Section 8.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Set up and evaluate the area between $x=\frac{1}{4}$ and $x=y^{2} - 2$ for $- \frac{3}{2}\le y\le \frac{3}{2}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{9}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: $\int (\text{right}-\text{left})\,dy$ uses horizontal slices.
U8-S3-NOTE-EX02
Section 8.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Set up and evaluate the area between $x=\frac{1}{4}$ and $x=y^{2} + 2 y - 1$ for $- \frac{5}{2}\le y\le \frac{1}{2}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{9}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: $\int (\text{right}-\text{left})\,dy$ uses horizontal slices.
U8-S3-NOTE-YTI02
Section 8.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

The curves $y=x^{3} - x$ and $y=x$ intersect at three points. Set up and evaluate the total bounded area between the outer intersections.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $-2$
Solution / scoring notes: Split where the upper/lower order changes at the middle intersection, then add positive areas.
U8-S3-NOTE-EX03
Section 8.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 3

Example

The curves $y=x \left(x^{2} - 2\right)$ and $y=x$ intersect at three points. Set up and evaluate the total bounded area between the outer intersections.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $- \frac{9}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Split where the upper/lower order changes at the middle intersection, then add positive areas.
U8-S3-NOTE-YTI03
Section 8.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

A region can be described either by vertical slices as two integrals or by horizontal slices as one integral. Which orientation is more efficient, and what must be true before choosing it?

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The horizontal-slice setup is more efficient if the left/right boundaries can be written as single functions over the full y-interval.
Solution / scoring notes: Efficiency depends on whether one orientation avoids unnecessary splitting while preserving correct bounding functions.
U8-S3-NOTE-EX04
Section 8.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 3

Example

A region can be described either by vertical slices as two integrals or by horizontal slices as one integral. Which orientation is more efficient, and what must be true before choosing it?

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: The horizontal-slice setup is more efficient if the left/right boundaries can be written as single functions over the full y-interval.
Solution / scoring notes: Efficiency depends on whether one orientation avoids unnecessary splitting while preserving correct bounding functions.
U8-S3-NOTE-YTI04
Section 8.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The figure shows $f$ and $g$. Over the bounded interval between their intersections, write the area integral and evaluate it for the plotted functions $f(x)=\frac{3}{2}$ and $g(x)=\frac{3 x^{2}}{2} - 6 x + 6$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: $\int_{1}^{3}(f-g)\,dx$ evaluates the region because f is above g there.

Section 8.4 - Volumes with Cross Sections

U8-S4-NOTE-EX01
Section 8.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The base region has vertical width $- \frac{3 x^{2}}{2} + 9 x - \frac{15}{2}$ for $1\le x\le 5$. Cross sections perpendicular to the x-axis are squares. Set up and evaluate the volume.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{384}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: $A(x)=[\text{side}]^2$ and $V=\int A(x)\,dx$.
U8-S4-NOTE-YTI01
Section 8.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The base-region width is $\frac{49}{16} - x^{2}$. Cross sections perpendicular to the x-axis are semicircles whose diameters lie in the base. Find the volume.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{16807 \pi}{7680}$
Solution / scoring notes: $A(x)=\frac{\pi}{8}d(x)^2$ because the stated base width is a diameter.
U8-S4-NOTE-EX02
Section 8.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The base-region width is $\frac{49}{32} - \frac{x^{2}}{2}$. Cross sections perpendicular to the x-axis are semicircles whose diameters lie in the base. Find the volume.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{16807 \pi}{30720}$
Solution / scoring notes: $A(x)=\frac{\pi}{8}d(x)^2$ because the stated base width is a diameter.
U8-S4-NOTE-YTI02
Section 8.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Each cross section perpendicular to the x-axis is a rectangle with base $- x^{2} + 2 x + \frac{9}{16}$ and height $- \frac{x^{2}}{2} + x + \frac{9}{32}$. Find the volume.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{625}{384}$
Solution / scoring notes: $A(x)=\text{base}\cdot\text{height}$ before integrating.
U8-S4-NOTE-EX03
Section 8.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Each cross section perpendicular to the x-axis is a rectangle with base $\frac{3 x \left(4 - x\right)}{4}$ and height $\frac{3 x \left(4 - x\right)}{8}$. Find the volume.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{48}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: $A(x)=\text{base}\cdot\text{height}$ before integrating.
U8-S4-NOTE-YTI03
Section 8.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Equilateral triangles are built perpendicular to the x-axis on base length $- x^{2} - 6 x - 8$. Set up the volume integral.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\int_{-4}^{-2}\frac{\sqrt3}{4}(- x^{2} - 6 x - 8)^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the equilateral-triangle area formula as the cross-sectional area function.
U8-S4-NOTE-EX04
Section 8.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Equilateral triangles are built perpendicular to the x-axis on base length $- \frac{3 x^{2}}{4} + 3 x - \frac{117}{64}$. Set up the volume integral.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\int_{\frac{3}{4}}^{\frac{13}{4}}\frac{\sqrt3}{4}(- \frac{3 x^{2}}{4} + 3 x - \frac{117}{64})^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the equilateral-triangle area formula as the cross-sectional area function.
U8-S4-NOTE-YTI04
Section 8.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The figure shows the base region bounded by $f(x)=-3$ and $g(x)=x^{2} - 4$. Square cross sections are perpendicular to the x-axis. Write the volume integral.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\int_{-1}^{1}(1 - x^{2})^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: The side length is the vertical distance between the curves; square that distance before integrating.

Section 8.5 - Volumes by Rotation

U8-S5-NOTE-EX01
Section 8.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The region under $y=3 x^{2} + 3$ from $x=0$ to $x=2$ is rotated about $y=-1$. The axis is below the region and forms discs. Set up and evaluate the volume.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{768 \pi}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: $V=\pi\int [\text{distance to axis}]^2\,dx$ with radius $3 x^{2} + 4$.
U8-S5-NOTE-YTI01
Section 8.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $0\le x\le 1$, the region between $y=4 - x$ and $y=1$ is rotated about $y=-1$. Set up and evaluate the washer volume.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{49 \pi}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: $V=\pi\int(R^2-r^2)\,dx$ with radii measured from the shifted axis.
U8-S5-NOTE-EX02
Section 8.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $0\le x\le 3$, the region between $y=6 - x$ and $y=1$ is rotated about $y=-2$. Set up and evaluate the washer volume.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $102 \pi$
Solution / scoring notes: $V=\pi\int(R^2-r^2)\,dx$ with radii measured from the shifted axis.
U8-S5-NOTE-YTI02
Section 8.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The region $0\le y\le 2$ lies between $x=6$ and $x=y^2$. It is rotated about $x=-2$. Write and evaluate a washer integral with respect to $y$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{1544 \pi}{15}$
Solution / scoring notes: $\pi\int[(\text{outer radius})^2-(\text{inner radius})^2]dy$ with horizontal slices.
U8-S5-NOTE-EX03
Section 8.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The region $0\le y\le 2$ lies between $x=7$ and $x=y^2$. It is rotated about $x=-1$. Write and evaluate a washer integral with respect to $y$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{1714 \pi}{15}$
Solution / scoring notes: $\pi\int[(\text{outer radius})^2-(\text{inner radius})^2]dy$ with horizontal slices.
U8-S5-NOTE-YTI03
Section 8.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For the curve $y=x + 3$ rotated about $y=-2$, compare the incorrect radius $x + 3$ with the correct radius expression.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $x + 5$
Solution / scoring notes: A radius is a distance from the curve to the axis, so subtract the axis coordinate.
U8-S5-NOTE-EX04
Section 8.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For the curve $y=x + 3$ rotated about $y=-2$, compare the incorrect radius $x + 3$ with the correct radius expression.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $x + 5$
Solution / scoring notes: A radius is a distance from the curve to the axis, so subtract the axis coordinate.
U8-S5-NOTE-YTI04
Section 8.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The figure shows a curve and the horizontal axis of rotation $y=0$. For the region under $f(x)=2 x^{2} + 1$ on $0\le x\le 3$, state the disc radius and write the volume integral.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $2 x^{2} + 1$; $\pi\int_0^{3}(2 x^{2} + 1)^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Measure the vertical distance from the function to the given axis.

2-5. Practice Sets 1-3 and Extra Practice

Practice Set 1

Section 8.1 - Average Value and Net Change

U8-S1-PS1-C01
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A quantity changes at rate $R(t)=t + 2$ units/hour. Find its net change from $t=4$ to $t=5$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{13}{2}$ units
Solution / scoring notes: $\int_a^b R(t)\,dt$ gives signed net change.
U8-S1-PS1-C02
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=x^{2} + 1$ on $[0,2]$, compute both the average value and the average rate of change and identify which uses a definite integral.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Average value $\frac{7}{3}$; average rate of change $2$.
Solution / scoring notes: Average value divides a definite integral by interval length; average rate of change uses endpoint function values.
U8-S1-PS1-C03
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A tank contains 50 liters at $t=0$. Its net rate is $R(t)=3 t + 3$ liters/hour. How much is in the tank at $t=2$?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $62$ liters
Solution / scoring notes: Final amount = initial amount + net change = 50 + $12$.
U8-S1-PS1-C04
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A rate is tabulated at three equally spaced times. Use the trapezoidal rule to estimate net change.
$t$012
$R(t)$4-11

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $1.5$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply two trapezoids with the common half-interval width and preserve the sign of the rate values.
U8-S1-PS1-C05
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 3
A student computes average value on $[a,b]$ as $\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$ and stops. Explain what is missing and why it matters.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{1}{b-a}\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Average value is the accumulated total divided by interval length; omitting the factor changes the quantity.
U8-S1-PS1-L01
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 1
A population changes at 8% of its current amount per hour. Write a differential equation.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $P'=0.08P$
Solution / scoring notes: Translate proportional growth directly into derivative form.
U8-S1-PS1-L02
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
A quantity has half-life 6. Find $k$ in $Q'=kQ$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-\ln 2/6$
Solution / scoring notes: $e^{kh}=1/2$ determines the proportionality constant.
U8-S1-PS1-L03
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Write the particular solution of $Q'=kQ$ with $k=\frac{3}{20}$ and $Q(0)=150$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $Q(t)=150e^{\frac{3}{20}t}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the exponential solution to a proportional-rate differential equation.
U8-S1-PS1-L04
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 1
For $Q'=kQ$ with $k=-0.1$, classify the model and state the units of $k$ if time is measured in days.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Exponential decay; per day.
Solution / scoring notes: The sign gives growth/decay and k has reciprocal-time units.
U8-S1-PS1-L05
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
A student proposes $Q(t)=Q_0e^{kt}$ as a solution to $Q'=kQ$. State the derivative check that verifies the model.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $Q'(t)=kQ_0e^{kt}=kQ(t)$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate and compare with the differential equation.
U8-S1-PS1-M01
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Evaluate $\int_{2}^{4}(2 x + 5)\,dx$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $22$
Solution / scoring notes: Use an antiderivative and evaluate upper minus lower.
U8-S1-PS1-M02
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
If $Q(t)$ is measured in liters and $t$ in minutes, state the units of $Q'(t)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: liters per minutes
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative units are output units per input unit.
U8-S1-PS1-M03

Simplify $3 x + 2$ for $x\ne0$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $3 x + 2$
Solution / scoring notes: Divide each numerator term by x.
U8-S1-PS1-M04
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
If $\int_0^{1} r(t)\,dt=-7$, evaluate $\int_{1}^0 r(t)\,dt$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $7$
Solution / scoring notes: Reversing bounds changes the sign.
U8-S1-PS1-M05
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
On an interval, $g'(x)$ is negative. What does this imply about $g$?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $g$ is decreasing.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the sign of the derivative.
U8-S1-PS1-M06
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to 1}\frac{x^2-1}{x-1}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor, cancel, and substitute.
U8-S1-PS1-M07
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Differentiate $f(x)=16 \left(x - 1\right)^{2}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $f'(x)=32 x - 32$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the chain rule.
U8-S1-PS1-M08
Section 8.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
Find a general antiderivative of $2 x^{2} + 2 x + 4$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{x \left(2 x^{2} + 3 x + 12\right)}{3}+C$
Solution / scoring notes: Integrate term by term and include the arbitrary constant.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

A student uses $\int_a^b v(t)\,dt$ for total distance even though the velocity changes sign. Explain the error and give the correct structure.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\int_a^b |v(t)|\,dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Total distance accumulates speed; split where velocity changes sign or integrate the absolute value.

Answer: $\int_{- \frac{5}{4}}^{\frac{5}{4}}\frac{\sqrt3}{4}(\frac{25}{32} - \frac{x^{2}}{2})^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the equilateral-triangle area formula as the cross-sectional area function.
U8-S4-PS2-C03

Answer: $\int_{- \frac{17}{4}}^{- \frac{7}{4}}(- \frac{x^{2}}{2} - 3 x - \frac{119}{32})^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: The side length is the vertical distance between the curves; square that distance before integrating.

Solution / scoring notes: The integral must accumulate cross-sectional area, not the unsquared side length.

Section 8.5 - Volumes by Rotation

U8-S5-PS2-C01
Section 8.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
The region $0\le y\le 1$ lies between $x=3$ and $x=y^2$. It is rotated about $x=-2$. Write and evaluate a washer integral with respect to $y$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{292 \pi}{15}$
Solution / scoring notes: $\pi\int[(\text{outer radius})^2-(\text{inner radius})^2]dy$ with horizontal slices.
U8-S5-PS2-C02
Section 8.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
For the curve $y=x + 3$ rotated about $y=-2$, compare the incorrect radius $x + 3$ with the correct radius expression.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $x + 5$
Solution / scoring notes: A radius is a distance from the curve to the axis, so subtract the axis coordinate.
U8-S5-PS2-C03
Section 8.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
The figure shows a curve and the horizontal axis of rotation $y=-2$. For the region under $f(x)=x^{2} + 2$ on $0\le x\le 3$, state the disc radius and write the volume integral.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $x^{2} + 4$; $\pi\int_0^{3}(x^{2} + 4)^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Measure the vertical distance from the function to the given axis.
U8-S5-PS2-C04
Section 8.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A washer setup uses $\pi\int_a^b(r^2-R^2)\,dx$ where $R$ is the outer radius and $r$ the inner radius. Explain why the order is wrong and repair it.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\pi\int_a^b(R^2-r^2)\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Washer area is outer-disc area minus inner-disc area and must be nonnegative.
U8-S5-PS2-C05
Section 8.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
The region under $y=3 x + 2$ from $x=0$ to $x=3$ is rotated about $y=0$. The axis is below the region and forms discs. Set up and evaluate the volume.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $147 \pi$
Solution / scoring notes: $V=\pi\int [\text{distance to axis}]^2\,dx$ with radius $3 x + 2$.
U8-S5-PS2-L01
Section 8.5 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 3

Answer: $V=\int_a^b [f(x)-g(x)]^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: The integral must accumulate cross-sectional area, not the unsquared side length.

Extra Practice

Section 8.1 - Average Value and Net Change

U8-S1-XP-C01
Section 8.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
A rate is tabulated at three equally spaced times. Use the trapezoidal rule to estimate net change.
$t$123
$R(t)$4-14

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $3$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply two trapezoids with the common half-interval width and preserve the sign of the rate values.
U8-S1-XP-C02
Section 8.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student computes average value on $[a,b]$ as $\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$ and stops. Explain what is missing and why it matters.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{1}{b-a}\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Average value is the accumulated total divided by interval length; omitting the factor changes the quantity.
U8-S1-XP-C03
Section 8.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Find the average value of $f(x)=x^{2} + 2 x + 1$ on $[0,1]$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{7}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: $f_{avg}=\frac{1}{b-a}\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$; substitute the interval and evaluate.
U8-S1-XP-C04
Section 8.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
A quantity changes at rate $R(t)=2 t - 1$ units/hour. Find its net change from $t=1$ to $t=4$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $12$ units
Solution / scoring notes: $\int_a^b R(t)\,dt$ gives signed net change.
U8-S1-XP-C05
Section 8.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=x^{2} + 2$ on $[0,3]$, compute both the average value and the average rate of change and identify which uses a definite integral.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Average value $5$; average rate of change $3$.
Solution / scoring notes: Average value divides a definite integral by interval length; average rate of change uses endpoint function values.
U8-S1-XP-L01
Section 8.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 1
For $Q'=kQ$ with $k=-0.25$, classify the model and state the units of $k$ if time is measured in days.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Exponential decay; per day.
Solution / scoring notes: The sign gives growth/decay and k has reciprocal-time units.
U8-S1-XP-L02
Section 8.1 | Extra Practice | Last | DOK 2
A student proposes $Q(t)=Q_0e^{kt}$ as a solution to $Q'=kQ$. State the derivative check that verifies the model.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $Q'(t)=kQ_0e^{kt}=kQ(t)$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate and compare with the differential equation.

A population changes at 8% of its current amount per hour. Write a differential equation.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $P'=0.08P$
Solution / scoring notes: Translate proportional growth directly into derivative form.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $-\ln 2/3$
Solution / scoring notes: $e^{kh}=1/2$ determines the proportionality constant.

Write the particular solution of $Q'=kQ$ with $k=- \frac{1}{5}$ and $Q(0)=120$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $Q(t)=120e^{- \frac{1}{5}t}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the exponential solution to a proportional-rate differential equation.

A student uses $\int_a^b v(t)\,dt$ for total distance even though the velocity changes sign. Explain the error and give the correct structure.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\int_a^b |v(t)|\,dt$
Solution / scoring notes: Total distance accumulates speed; split where velocity changes sign or integrate the absolute value.

Section 8.4 - Volumes with Cross Sections

Section 8.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Solution / scoring notes: The side length is the vertical distance between the curves; square that distance before integrating.

Answer: $V=\int_a^b [f(x)-g(x)]^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: The integral must accumulate cross-sectional area, not the unsquared side length.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $\frac{96}{5}$

6. Check Your Understanding

Section 8.1 - Average Value and Net Change

U8-S1-CYU-C01
Section 8.1 | Cyu | Current | DOK 2
A quantity changes at rate $R(t)=t - 1$ units/hour. Find its net change from $t=3$ to $t=5$.

Clearly mark the quantity that answers the question.

Answer: $6$ units
Solution / scoring notes: $\int_a^b R(t)\,dt$ gives signed net change.
U8-S1-CYU-C02
Section 8.1 | Cyu | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=x^{2} + 1$ on $[0,2]$, compute both the average value and the average rate of change and identify which uses a definite integral.

Clearly mark the quantity that answers the question.

Answer: Average value $\frac{7}{3}$; average rate of change $2$.
Solution / scoring notes: Average value divides a definite integral by interval length; average rate of change uses endpoint function values.
U8-S1-CYU-L01
Section 8.1 | Cyu | Last | DOK 2
A quantity has half-life 8. Find $k$ in $Q'=kQ$.

Clearly mark the quantity that answers the question.

Answer: $-\ln 2/8$
Solution / scoring notes: $e^{kh}=1/2$ determines the proportionality constant.

Solution / scoring notes: The integral must accumulate cross-sectional area, not the unsquared side length.

9. Performance / Future Tasks

Section 8.1 - Average Value and Net Change

U8-S1-PERF01
Section 8.1 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 3

Accumulation Quantity Audit

A pump rate is $R(t)=6-2t$ liters/minute on $[0,3]$, and the tank starts with 40 liters. Determine the net change, final amount, and average value of the rate. A student claims all three quantities are represented by the same definite integral. Critique the claim, identify the formula for each requested quantity, and explain the units of each result.

Support each conclusion with the integral, geometric, graphical, or contextual evidence requested.

Expected evidence: A complete response distinguishes $\int_0^3R(t)dt$ as net change, 40 plus that integral as final amount, and $\frac13\int_0^3R(t)dt$ as average rate value.
Scoring criteria: Score correct formulas, units, interpretation, and a specific critique of the student claim.
Estimated student time: 15-20 minutes
Answer: A complete response distinguishes $\int_0^3R(t)dt$ as net change, 40 plus that integral as final amount, and $\frac13\int_0^3R(t)dt$ as average rate value.
Solution / scoring notes: A complete response distinguishes $\int_0^3R(t)dt$ as net change, 40 plus that integral as final amount, and $\frac13\int_0^3R(t)dt$ as average rate value.

Section 8.2 - Motion with Integrals

U8-S2-PERF01
Section 8.2 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Motion Accumulation Investigation

A particle has velocity $v(t)=t^2-4t+3$ meters/second for $0\le t\le5$ and starts at $s(0)=2$ meters. Determine all direction-change times, displacement, total distance, and final position. Then compare the four quantities and revise this statement if needed: “because the displacement is small, the particle barely moved.” Support the revision with integral evidence.

Support each conclusion with the integral, geometric, graphical, or contextual evidence requested.

Expected evidence: The response identifies velocity zeros, uses signed accumulation for displacement, absolute accumulation for distance, adds the initial position for final position, and revises the claim using total-distance evidence.
Scoring criteria: Full credit requires interval/sign analysis, correct integrals, distinct interpretations, units, and evidence-based claim revision.
Estimated student time: 25-30 minutes
Answer: The response identifies velocity zeros, uses signed accumulation for displacement, absolute accumulation for distance, adds the initial position for final position, and revises the claim using total-distance evidence.
Solution / scoring notes: The response identifies velocity zeros, uses signed accumulation for displacement, absolute accumulation for distance, adds the initial position for final position, and revises the claim using total-distance evidence.

Section 8.3 - Area Between Curves

U8-S3-PERF01
Section 8.3 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Area Modeling Performance

Consider the region bounded by $y=x^2$ and $y=2x+3$. Find the intersections. Build a valid vertical-slice area model and then investigate whether a horizontal-slice model can represent the entire region with one integral. If it cannot, split it correctly. Compare the two approaches for efficiency and revise any initial claim that ignores a change in the left/right boundary.

Support each conclusion with the integral, geometric, graphical, or contextual evidence requested.

Expected evidence: The response finds the two intersections, gives a correct dx model, derives the required inverse/left-right descriptions for dy, and explains any necessary split.
Scoring criteria: Score correct geometry, bounds, both orientations, efficiency comparison, and explicit model revision when a bounding relation changes.
Estimated student time: 25-30 minutes
Answer: The response finds the two intersections, gives a correct dx model, derives the required inverse/left-right descriptions for dy, and explains any necessary split.
Solution / scoring notes: The response finds the two intersections, gives a correct dx model, derives the required inverse/left-right descriptions for dy, and explains any necessary split.

Section 8.4 - Volumes with Cross Sections

U8-S4-PERF01
Section 8.4 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Cross Section Design Task

The base region is bounded by $y=4-x^2$ and $y=0$ for $-2\le x\le2$. Design two solids on this same base: Solid A has square cross sections perpendicular to the x-axis; Solid B has semicircular cross sections with diameter in the base. Build both area functions, verify their square-unit dimensions, write both volume integrals, and compare which solid has larger volume without relying only on a decimal approximation.

Support each conclusion with the integral, geometric, graphical, or contextual evidence requested.

Expected evidence: The response uses side/diameter $4-x^2$, areas $(4-x^2)^2$ and $\frac\pi8(4-x^2)^2$, and compares the constant multipliers.
Scoring criteria: Full credit requires two valid area functions, dimension check, volume models, and a justified comparison.
Estimated student time: 20-25 minutes
Answer: The response uses side/diameter $4-x^2$, areas $(4-x^2)^2$ and $\frac\pi8(4-x^2)^2$, and compares the constant multipliers.
Solution / scoring notes: The response uses side/diameter $4-x^2$, areas $(4-x^2)^2$ and $\frac\pi8(4-x^2)^2$, and compares the constant multipliers.

Section 8.5 - Volumes by Rotation

U8-S5-PERF01
Section 8.5 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Rotation Model Performance

The region bounded by $y=x+1$, $y=1$, $x=0$, and $x=2$ is rotated first about $y=0$ and then about $y=-2$. Build washer models for both rotations, compare the inner and outer radii, and explain how shifting the axis changes the volume. A student reuses the first pair of radii for the second rotation; diagnose and repair that model.

Support each conclusion with the integral, geometric, graphical, or contextual evidence requested.

Expected evidence: The response measures both radii from each stated axis, writes two washer integrals, and explains why every radius increases when the axis moves downward.
Scoring criteria: Score correct radius distances, washer order, two complete models, comparison, and repair of the reused-radius error.
Estimated student time: 20-25 minutes
Answer: The response measures both radii from each stated axis, writes two washer integrals, and explains why every radius increases when the axis moves downward.
Solution / scoring notes: The response measures both radii from each stated axis, writes two washer integrals, and explains why every radius increases when the axis moves downward.

DOK 1 - Average Value and Net Change Check

U8-FA-D1-01-V1
Section 8.1 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 1

Average Value and Net Change Check

A rate $R(t)$ is integrated over time. What does $\int_a^bR(t)dt$ represent?

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: Net change in the underlying quantity.
Solution / scoring notes: A definite integral of a rate accumulates signed change.
U8-FA-D1-01-V2
Section 8.1 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 1

Average Value and Net Change Check

State one difference between average value and average rate of change.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: Average value uses a definite integral over the interval; average rate of change uses endpoint function values divided by interval length.
Solution / scoring notes: The two quantities average different mathematical objects.
U8-FA-D1-01-V3
Section 8.1 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 1

Average Value and Net Change Check

Which formula gives the average value of $f$ on $[a,b]$?

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: $\frac{1}{b-a}\int_a^b f(x)\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Average value divides accumulated function values by interval length.

DOK 2 - Volume Setup Application

Volume Setup Application

Square cross sections have side $- \frac{3 x^{2}}{2} - 6 x - \frac{21}{8}$ on $- \frac{7}{2}\le x\le - \frac{1}{2}$. Set up the volume.

Answer: $\int_{- \frac{7}{2}}^{- \frac{1}{2}}(- \frac{3 x^{2}}{2} - 6 x - \frac{21}{8})^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Square the side length to obtain cross-sectional area.

Solution / scoring notes: Radius is distance from the curve to the axis.

Volume Setup Application

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: $\int_{\frac{7}{4}}^{\frac{17}{4}}(- \frac{5 x^{2}}{4} + \frac{15 x}{2} - \frac{595}{64})^2\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Square the side length to obtain cross-sectional area.

DOK 3 - Motion Quantity Strategy

U8-FA-D3-01-V1
Section 8.2 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Motion Quantity Strategy

A particle changes direction once on $[0,6]$. A prompt asks “how far from its starting position is the particle at $t=6$?” Compare displacement, total distance, and final position as possible targets. Choose the correct quantity and justify.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: Use displacement magnitude if “how far from its starting position” means separation from the initial location; total distance measures path length, while final position also requires the initial coordinate.
Solution / scoring notes: The wording determines whether the target is signed change, path length, or absolute location.

DOK 3 - Area Orientation Decision

U8-FA-D3-02-V1
Section 8.3 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Area Orientation Decision

A bounded region would require three vertical-slice integrals but only one horizontal-slice integral after solving the boundaries for $x$. Which orientation is more efficient? State one condition that must still be checked before using it.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: Use horizontal slices if the right and left boundaries are valid single functions across the full y-range; verify the boundary order and y-bounds.
Solution / scoring notes: Method choice must be justified by geometric structure, not merely by fewer symbols.

DOK 3 - Cross-Section Model Critique

U8-FA-D3-03-V1
Section 8.4 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Cross-Section Model Critique

A base-region slice has length $s(x)$. Cross sections are rectangles whose height is $3s(x)$. A student proposes $A(x)=3s(x)$. Critique and repair the model.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: $A(x)=3s(x)^2$
Solution / scoring notes: Area is base times height, so both length factors must be included.

DOK 3 - Rotation Setup Defense

U8-FA-D3-04-V1
Section 8.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Rotation Setup Defense

A region between $y=f(x)$ and $y=g(x)$ is rotated about $y=-3$, with $f(x)\ge g(x)\gt -3$. Defend the correct washer radii and explain why using $f$ and $g$ themselves is wrong.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Answer: $R=f(x)+3,\quad r=g(x)+3$
Solution / scoring notes: Radii are distances to the shifted axis; function values alone measure from the x-axis.

Section 8.2 | Formative Assessment | Performance | DOK 4

Motion Accumulation Investigation

The figure gives a piecewise-linear velocity model over the displayed time interval. Determine displacement and total distance, identify each direction-change time, and write a two-sentence interpretation explaining how two motions can have the same displacement but different total distance. Then test your interpretation against this graph and revise it if necessary.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Expected evidence: Evidence aligned to the named Unit 8 formative family.
Scoring criteria: Award credit for complete model construction, comparison, validation, and revision.
Answer: Displacement $- \frac{33}{2}$; total distance $\frac{49}{2}$.
Solution / scoring notes: Use signed and absolute geometric accumulation, locate velocity zeros, and connect the numerical comparison to the path interpretation.

Cross-Section Design Task

The figure shows a base region with vertical width $- \frac{x^{2}}{2} - 2 x - \frac{3}{2}$. Design two solids on this base: one with square cross sections and one with equilateral-triangle cross sections. Build both area functions, write both volume models, compare their volumes symbolically, and verify that each integrand has square units before integration.

Use only the supplied assessment evidence and justify where requested.

Expected evidence: Evidence aligned to the named Unit 8 formative family.
Scoring criteria: Award credit for complete model construction, comparison, validation, and revision.
Answer: Square area $\frac{\left(x^{2} + 4 x + 3\right)^{2}}{4}$; equilateral-triangle area $\frac{\sqrt{3} \left(x^{2} + 4 x + 3\right)^{2}}{16}$.
Solution / scoring notes: The same base width enters two different area formulas; compare the constant geometric factors.

What is the average value of $x^{2} + 1$ on $[0,4]$?
  1. $\frac{19}{3}$
  2. $\frac{76}{3}$
  3. $4$
  4. $5.0$

Use AP-calculus notation, show integral setup clearly, and include units or contextual interpretation when relevant.

Answer: A. $\frac{19}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use one over interval length times the definite integral.

  2. $12$
  3. $4$
  4. $7$

Answer: B. $12$

U8-SUM-V1-Q04
Section 8.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 8 Summative - Version 1

A quantity starts at 20 units and changes at rate $R(t)=3 t^{2} - 2 t + 1$. On $0\le t\le 2$, find the net change, final amount, and average value of the rate, with units described symbolically.

Use AP-calculus notation, show integral setup clearly, and include units or contextual interpretation when relevant.

Answer: Net change $6$; final amount $26$; average rate value $3$.
Solution / scoring notes: Use one definite integral for net change, add the initial value for final amount, and divide by interval length for average value.

U8-SUM-V1-Q09

Unit 8 Summative - Version 1

Which integral gives the area between $y=\frac{41}{16}$ and $y=x^{2} - 6 x + 10$ between their intersections?
  1. $\int_{\frac{7}{4}}^{\frac{17}{4}}(x^{2} - 6 x + \frac{119}{16})\,dx$
  2. $\int_{\frac{7}{4}}^{\frac{17}{4}}(- x^{2} + 6 x - \frac{119}{16})\,dx$
  3. $\int_{\frac{7}{4}}^{\frac{17}{4}}(\frac{41}{16})\,dx$
  4. $\int_{\frac{7}{4}}^{\frac{17}{4}}(x^{2} - 6 x + 10)\,dx$

Use AP-calculus notation, show integral setup clearly, and include units or contextual interpretation when relevant.

Answer: B. $\int_{\frac{7}{4}}^{\frac{17}{4}}(- x^{2} + 6 x - \frac{119}{16})\,dx$
Solution / scoring notes: Use upper minus lower over the intersection bounds.

U8-SUM-V1-Q12

Unit 8 Summative - Version 1

A region is bounded horizontally by $x=\frac{3 y^{2}}{2} + 6 y + 7$ and $x=\frac{35}{8}$ for $- \frac{7}{2}\le y\le - \frac{1}{2}$. Write and evaluate the area integral with respect to y. Explain why a dx setup would be less direct.

Use AP-calculus notation, show integral setup clearly, and include units or contextual interpretation when relevant.

Answer: $\frac{27}{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use right minus left in dy; a dx description would require solving/splitting boundary branches.
U8-SUM-V1-Q13

Unit 8 Summative - Version 1

Square cross sections have side $f(x)-g(x)$. Which expression is the cross-sectional area?
  1. $f(x)-g(x)$
  2. $2[f(x)-g(x)]$
  3. $\pi[f(x)-g(x)]^2$
  4. $[f(x)-g(x)]^2$

Use AP-calculus notation, show integral setup clearly, and include units or contextual interpretation when relevant.

Answer: D. $[f(x)-g(x)]^2$
Solution / scoring notes: Square area is side squared.
U8-SUM-V1-Q14
Section 8.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 8 Summative - Version 1

Semicircular cross sections have diameter $d(x)$. Which expression is the area function?
  1. $\frac{\pi}{8}d(x)^2$
  2. $\pi d(x)^2$
  3. $\frac{\pi}{4}d(x)^2$
  4. $\frac12d(x)^2$

Use AP-calculus notation, show integral setup clearly, and include units or contextual interpretation when relevant.

Answer: A. $\frac{\pi}{8}d(x)^2$
Solution / scoring notes: Radius is d/2 and a semicircle has half the area of a full circle.

What is the radius from $y=x + 3$ to the axis $y=-2$?
  1. $x + 3$

Answer: $\frac{1647 \pi}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: Measure radius as vertical distance from the curve to each axis and square it in the disc-area formula.
